# Co-Optimisation Audit Implementation

Status, IMPLEMENTED 2026-10-07, applied as the byte identical copy of the verified sandbox and validated in the tree by the unit suite, appendices A to C and the run of section 9.8, whose outcome `../context/co_optimisation_audit.md` section 12 records. Revised and verified 2026-10-06, revised again and verified 2026-10-07. The revisions answer two reviews, recorded item by item in section 0.3. The nominal fold of both robots now lives in the body frames of every Isaac Lab document, which retires the separate quadruped co-optimisation asset and the legacy scheme, all four design generators are selectable, the categorical search gains its random phase and swaps its designs in place like the others, the population checks move to `train.py`, each robot's co-optimisation task takes reward and curriculum classes of its own, and the nominal pose is observed from the joint frames that hold it. Every edit below was applied to a sandbox copy of the working tree and exercised there, by the unit suite, by three simulator checks in the Isaac Lab container and by end to end training runs of every generator on both robots (section 9), and the source tree itself was not modified. The plan implements every finding that [../context/co_optimisation_audit.md](../context/co_optimisation_audit.md) marks Apply or Plan, so that the quadruped and the TRON1 solefoot biped both co-optimise in one run.

## 0. How to read this plan

The audit is the source of every requirement here, and this plan is its executable form. It continues the audit's investigation where the audit stopped, at the point of writing code, and it records in section 1 every place where the code contradicted or refined the audit, since several of those refinements change what must be written. The work is then laid out in eight phases, each of which states what the investigation found, the architecture chosen and why, the edits as unified diffs taken verbatim from the tested sandbox, the order in which to apply them, how they were verified and what the phase delivers. Each phase closes with a review against the audit, a table that walks the audit's own statements for the items the phase covers and marks each as adhered to or as a recorded divergence with its reason.

Every diff applies to the working tree as it stood on 2026-10-06, uncommitted changes included, and was generated by `git diff --no-index` between a snapshot of that tree and the sandbox. A file that several phases edit has its hunks distributed among them, each hunk shown exactly once, so the diffs are applied by hand, file by file in the order of section 9.7, rather than fed to `git apply` phase by phase. Line numbers in the hunk headers and in the prose are those of the snapshot. Following the convention of [README.md](README.md), an edit is located by its symbol and the quoted context, the numbers being corroboration rather than address.

Two conventions govern the code. A comment in added code carries only what the code cannot say, a reason, a unit, a citation or a consequence, and a docstring is kept where it states a contract the signature does not. A code block that quotes a file which itself contains a fence, such as the README, is opened with one backtick more than the longest fence inside it, so that the block's end is unambiguous to a renderer and to a reader.

### 0.2 Verification environment

The training container `isaac-lab-base-shreyas-gpu0` mounts this repository at `/workspace/isaaclab/biped` and runs Isaac Sim 5.1 with Isaac Lab 0.54. The sandbox was a copy of the working tree at `/tmp/copt_sandbox` inside that container, placed ahead of the installed packages on `PYTHONPATH`, with the real `cmaes` 0.13.1 installed to a private directory, so nothing the plan proposes touched the tree or the container's site packages. Training runs wrote their logs under `/tmp/copt_work`, away from the user's log tree. Two facts about that container bear on implementation and are stated once here.

## 1. What this pass found that the audit did not

The audit's arithmetic for the pose was computed in plain Python from the builders' constants and its claims about the importer were not read. Writing the code exposed the following, every one of which is either measured in the container or read in a cited source.

| # | finding | evidence | effect on the plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The importer authors a revolute joint's `physics:localRot0` as the child body's quat composed with the rotation that aligns the joint axis with local x, and that alignment alone as `physics:localRot1`. A fixed joint takes the bare body quat and an identity `localRot1`. Writing the body quat into `localRot0` as the audit's section 4.9 proposes would turn every knee about local x. | `/ws/IsaacSim/source/extensions/isaacsim.asset.importer.mjcf/plugins/isaacsim.asset.importer.mjcf/MjcfImporter.cpp`, read line by line in section 4.1, and the converted USD of section 9.2, where `localRot0 = body quat x localRot1` holds to 5.4e-7 for all 14 pose joints of both robots | phase 5 composes the body quat with the joint's own `localRot1`, one rule for both kinds |
| 2 | The biped donor's ankle sits 13.99 mm ahead of its hip pitch axis, not the 25.2 mm of the audit's sections 4.3 and 4.5. The audit's chain ignores the shank to ankle clearance and the hip axis offset, and its fitted constant 0.290497 assumes the TRON1 housings. | forward kinematics of the emitted donor document gives 13.987 mm and 0.804361 m, section 9.1 | the solve and the checks use the exact sagittal chain of the emitted document, built from the builder's own helpers, with the target taken from the donor at construction rather than hard coded |

The audit's premise that 24 is the biped's count holds for the PF, WF, Berkeley, SD_BRS1 and KScale runners (`:26`, `:59`, `:211`, `:250`, `:288`, `:333`) and not for SF. | the cited lines | the biped's `CoptCurriculumCfg` is written in iterations x 25 too, the plain SF tasks keeping their schedule, section 8 |

With the fold in the body frames `default_joint_pos` is zero, so the nominal pose survives only in the joint frames on the stage and in the link kinematics, where a child turns against its parent by `R_y(fold + s q)` with `s` the sign of the joint axis, itself a stage fact. | the cited file, and `grep -rn "localRot\|joint_frame" isaaclab/` returning nothing | `robot_nominal_pose` reads the frames on the stage, once per initialisation of the articulation, section 7 |

## 2. Phase 1, the entry point, the four generators and the dependency blockers (B4, B5, B1, B3, B2)

### 2.1 Investigation outcome

Five defects stop or silently corrupt a run before any design question arises, and none of them needs a design decision of its own. B4 is confirmed in the training container, where `cmaes` is absent and the two packages the run imports are stale copies (section 0.2). The README's install line for `design_generator` names `co_optimisation/co_optimisation/runners/design_generator` (`README.md:59`), a path that no longer exists since the submodule moved to `design_generator/`, so a fresh install following it fails. B5, B1 and B2 stand exactly as the audit records them.

B3's arithmetic stands with one correction, 6000 % 256 = 112 for the quadruped, not 96, and 4096 % 256 = 0 for the biped. Every pathway that maps a design to its environments does so by `env_id % population`, the runner's round robin (`copt_on_policy_runner.py:744`), the in place editor and its read back (`update.py`, `apply_link_length_params` and `assert_link_lengths_applied`), the actuator overwrite (`respawn.py`, `apply_actuator_params`) and the multi asset spawner, and fitness is the mean return per design, so a population that does not divide the scene ranks designs on unequal evidence. The population a generator produces is fixed when it is constructed. `RandomDesignGenerator` builds `num_individuals` designs every generation (`usd_generator.py:502-503`), and the three CMA-ES generators build `late_start_prior_pop_size` designs while their late start flag is up and `num_individuals` after it (`usd_generator.py:632-636`), the launcher passing `env.num_envs / 4` for the first (`train.py:259`). Both numbers are therefore known where the generator is built, before the runner exists, and the check belongs there. A check per generation inside the runner would re-prove a constant at every swap, and the first draft's assertion in the runner's constructor would also have refused `play.py`, which builds `CoptOnPolicyRunner` with 256 individuals (`play.py:1084`) over the 32 environments of every copt Play configuration (`quadruped_pointfoot_env_cfg.py:169`, `limx_solefoot_env_cfg.py:173`).

Only two of the four generators of `usd_generator.py` are reachable today, `GrowingDesignDistCMAESDesignGenerator` under `--design_search length` and `CatCMAESDesignGenerator` under `cat` (`train.py:57-68`, `:231-262`). The three CMA-ES classes share one constructor, so one call serves them. `RandomDesignGenerator` takes the base constructor alone and has neither `toggle_late_start` nor `get_state` (section 1, finding 17). The runner calls `toggle_late_start` only under `self._ea_late_start > 0` (`copt_on_policy_runner.py:419-422`) and treats every generation at or after `ea_late_start` as a search generation (`:682`), so a random search launched with `ea_late_start = -1` draws a fresh population at every generation, through the in place route since its size never changes, and logs a fitness that nothing reads. Its checkpoint needs the two stateless methods this phase adds to the base class.

The audit's step 7 asks for a validation run at a reduced `ea_update_interval`, which `train.py` cannot do today, the three schedule constants being literals (`train.py:222-226`). Three command line flags whose defaults are those literals make the validation run of phase 8 possible without editing the launcher, and change nothing for a launch that names none of them.

### 2.2 Architecture and rationale

| `--design_search` | class | before `ea_late_start` | after it | populations | swap route |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `length`, the default | `GrowingDesignDistCMAESDesignGenerator` | a uniform prior that widens from 5 per cent of the box to all of it | CMA-ES over the four parameters of phase 4 | `num_envs / 4`, then `num_individuals` | in place, one respawn at the late start |
| `cmaes` | `CMAESDesignGenerator` | uniform over the whole box | CMA-ES | the same | the same |
| `cat` | `CatCMAESDesignGenerator` | the widening prior, every joint's actuator uniform over the shelf | `CatCMAwM` over the four parameters and one category per joint | the same | respawn at every generation, section 3 |
| `random` | `RandomDesignGenerator` | none, `ea_late_start = -1` | uniform over the whole box at every generation, fitness logged and unused | `num_individuals` | in place |

| item | design | rationale |
|---|---|---|
| B4 | declare `cma`, `cmaes>=0.11.0` and `pandas` in `co_optimisation/setup.py`, correct the README to `pip install -e design_generator co_optimisation` | `design_generator` is a package of this repository and not a distribution, so it is installed beside `co_optimisation` and not declared by it |
| B5 | `choices` on `--policy-type` | the six values are the ones `train.py:206-216` branches on |
| B1 | a substring map from task id to family, refusing an unknown task | substrings resolve the Flat, Rough and Play ids of both families, `robots/__init__.py:389-414` and `:650-678` |
| B3 | `train.py` raises unless `num_envs` is a multiple of every population the chosen generator produces, and the quadruped copt task takes 6144 environments | the populations are constructor arguments, the review's item 3, and 6144 = 24 x 256 = 4 x 1536 is the nearest multiple above the 6000 the plain task was tuned at, set on `QuadrupedPFCoptEnvCfg` alone |
| the four generators | one `--design_search` choice per class, the three CMA-ES classes built by one call, the random search without a late start | the review's item 4, and `length` and `cat` keep their meaning, so every existing launch is unchanged |
| B2 | save `terrain_levels` as None on a plane and skip it on restore | `terrain_levels` exists only after `_compute_env_origins_curriculum` (`terrain_importer.py:347`) |
| checkpoints of the random search | `get_state` returning `{}` and `load_state` doing nothing on `DesignGeneratorBase` | the runner's save and load are written against the generator interface, section 1 finding 17 |
| validation flags | `--copt_num_individuals`, `--ea_update_interval`, `--ea_late_start`, defaults 256, 480 and 12000 | the audit's step 7 |

### 2.3 Edits

B4, `co_optimisation/setup.py:12`.

```diff
@@ -9,5 +9,9 @@ setup(
     packages=find_packages(),
```

### 2.4 Implementation instructions

1. Apply the `setup.py` and README hunks, then reinstall both packages editable in the training container (section 9.7 step 1) and run `python -c "import cma, cmaes, pandas, design_generator"`.
2. Apply the `cli_args.py` hunk and confirm `python train.py --policy-type copt` is refused by argparse.
3. Apply the two `train.py` hunks above. The third, the `copt_agent.yaml` dump, belongs to phase 7.
4. Add `get_state` and `load_state` to `DesignGeneratorBase`, in the same pass as the rest of `usd_generator.py` (section 9.7 step 4).
5. Apply the `num_envs=6144` line with phase 6's `QuadrupedPFCoptEnvCfg` hunk, and the two `env_state.py` hunks.

### 2.5 Verification and outcome

| check | result |
|---|---|
| the quadruped copt task reaches its own package | every quadruped run of section 9.4 emitted quadruped documents and initialised the articulation at `/trunk/trunk` |
| an unequal split is refused before the runner is built | `--num_envs 250` against 32 designs raised `ValueError: --num_envs 250 is not a multiple of the 62 designs a generation of --design_search length carries.`, the random phase's 250 // 4 = 62 being the first population checked, section 9.4 |
| every generator runs on both robots | `length`, `cmaes`, `cat` and `random`, eight runs, section 9.4 |
| the random search saves and resumes its checkpoint | the random runs wrote `model_4.pt` and `model_8.pt` with the stateless pair |
| B2 on a plane | `Isaac-Quadruped-Copt-Flat-v0` saved `model_0.pt`, section 9.4 |

The outcome is a launcher that reaches the right family and any of the four searches, refuses a typo and an unequal split before a single document is emitted, saves on a plane, and installs from a fresh clone.

### 2.6 Review against the audit

| audit statement | plan | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| B1, `_FAMILY_BY_TASK` with substring keys `"Isaac-Quadruped-Copt"` and `"Isaac-Limx-SF-Copt"`, refuse an unknown task (section 3) | the same map and refusal, written as `next(...)` over the same loop | adhered |
| B1, the map carries the family alone, the shelf being the family's (section 10) | no catalogue argument remains, phase 2 | adhered |
| B2, `getattr` on capture and a guarded copy on restore (section 3) | identical | adhered |
| B2, `push_robot` and `base_velocity` assumptions recorded, not repaired | untouched | adhered |
| B3, an assertion and not a warning, `num_envs % num_individuals == 0`, in the runner's constructor, plus a per generation check in `_update_morphology` (section 3) | a `ValueError` in `train.py` over both populations of the chosen generator, nothing in the runner | divergence by the review's item 3, the populations being fixed at construction, and the check covers the random phase the audit's two assertions covered between them |
| B3, `num_envs=6144` on `QuadrupedPFCoptEnvCfg` (section 3) | identical | adhered |
| B4, `install_requires` with `cma`, `cmaes>=0.11.0`, `pandas`, design_generator installed separately (section 3) | identical, with the README path corrected | adhered, the README correction added because the documented install path no longer exists |
| B5, `choices` with the six values (section 3) | identical | adhered |
| order of work step 7, a validation run at a reduced `ea_update_interval` (section 8) | three flags defaulting to today's constants | adhered, the flags are an addition the audit's step requires |
| the generators reachable from the launcher | all four, an addition of the review's item 4 | addition |

## 3. Phase 2, the actuator shelf and the categorical search (B6, B7, X5)

### 3.1 Investigation outcome

The completeness count of B7 was re-derived against `is_sim_complete` as written below, by calling `sim_params()` on every entry of both live catalogues. Three quadruped entries (`A1-ABAD`, `A1-HIP`, `A1-KNEE`) and four biped entries (`TRON1-ABAD`, `TRON1-HIP`, `TRON1-KNEE`, `TRON1-ANKLE`) pass, and the other eleven of each fail, exactly as the audit records. The biped's passing set is the shortlist `train.py:245-247` passes today, so B6 together with B7 reproduces the biped's categorical shelf exactly and gives the quadruped the three entries it never reached.

One detail of the catalogue module changes where the filter lives. `set_active_catalog` rebinds the module global `ACTUATOR_CATALOG` (`quadruped/actuator.py:51-64`), so the audit's `SIM_ACTUATOR_CATALOG`, a list bound at import, would go stale after a swap. The filter is therefore a function, `sim_actuator_catalog()`, that reads the live global at call time. The tests at `co_optimisation/tests/design_generator_test.py:433-454` exercise the two arguments B6 deletes and are replaced in the same pass.

The categorical search needs a random phase, so that the policy entering the search can already walk on every actuator the search may pick, and the generator already has one. While its late start flag is up, `CatCMAESDesignGenerator._generate_individual` draws the four continuous parameters from the same widening prior the growing generator uses and draws each joint's actuator uniformly from the shelf, `self.cat_names[rng.integers(len(self.cat_names))]` for every group, from one generator seeded by the generation and the individual (`usd_generator.py:910-916`), and the inherited `generate_population` builds `late_start_prior_pop_size` such designs (`:632-634`). What prevents the phase from running is the launcher alone, which builds the generator with `late_start=False` (`train.py:243`) while telling the runner that the late start falls at iteration 12000, the defect of section 1 finding 14. Built with the late start schedule of the other CMA-ES generators, the categorical search spends its random phase over every actuator on every joint while the lengths and the pose widen from the donor, then hands over to `CatCMAwM`, whose categorical marginals start uniform but for the baseline's own pick, which holds half the mass (`usd_generator.py:838-846`). The first draft's alternative, `ea_late_start = -1` and CMA-ES from the first generation, is withdrawn by the review's item 5.

Every categorical generation, random or searched, carries actuator picks, and the second draft respawned every one of them because the in place editor could not write what a pick moves (section 1, finding 15). The second review's item 2 asked what blocks the edit, and the answer is the editor alone. A pick changes four things in the emitted document, read from the builders and confirmed by `test_the_payload_covers_everything_an_actuator_pick_moves`, which compares every body of the document under every pick of every group with the donor's.

| what a pick moves | quadruped | biped | where the importer authors it |
|---|---|---|---|
| the housing cylinder's radius and half length | fixed sizes, `ABD_RADIUS` and the like (`quadruped/builder.py:102-107`) | the catalogue's diameter and length (`biped/builder.py:185-188`) | `radius` and `height` of the `UsdGeomCylinder` in the housing's visual and collision prototypes, the referencing `Xform` carrying the axis alignment and a unit scale (`MjcfUsd.cpp:799-818`, `MjcfImporter.cpp:428-444`) |
| the housing's mass and inertia | every housing | every housing | `physics:mass` and `physics:diagonalInertia` on the body prim, the principal axes fixed by the document |
| the placement of a body | none | the abduction housing from the radius and half length of its own unit, the hip housing from those of the abduction and hip units, the knee housing and the thigh from the half lengths of the hip and knee units (`_cluster_layout`, `biped/builder.py:287-320`) | `physics:localPos0` of the body's joint, a welded body's fixed joint named after it, as the dump of a converted biped shows for `abad_R_act` and `thigh_R_Link` |
| the sole below the ankle housing | none | the foot box and its centre of mass from the ankle unit's radius (`biped/builder.py:380-381`) | the box `Xform`'s translate and `physics:centerOfMass`, as for the links |

Nothing on that list needs a prim the design's document lacks, so the editor that already writes boxes, masses, centres of mass and joint frames on the source layer of each attribute writes them all. The one new write is the cylinder's, whose size lives on the cylinder prim rather than on a scale, and which the editor now finds by structure as it finds the box. Every other placement a pick moves is the `localPos0` the editor writes for a link's child already, and the abduction housing, whose parent is the trunk with two such children, is placed from its own entry. A pick's simulator parameters, its armature, gains and limits, never depended on the document on either route. Isaac Lab writes every joint's gains, limits, armature and friction from the actuator configuration at each initialisation of the articulation (`articulation.py:1759-1782`), over whatever drive the importer authored from the document's motor, and `apply_actuator_params` then writes each design's values per environment, after the respawn and after the in place reset alike.

### 3.2 Architecture and rationale

```
design_generator/common/actuator_catalog.py    is_sim_complete(entry)        the rule, beside SIM_PARAM_KEYS
design_generator/{quadruped,biped}/actuator.py sim_actuator_catalog()        the live catalogue filtered by the rule
co_optimisation/runners/usd_generator.py       Family.catalog()              the shelf every search draws from
                                               Family.full_catalog()         every entry, for accounting
                                               CatCMAESDesignGenerator       inherits CMAESDesignGenerator.__init__
                                                 random phase                widening prior, actuator uniform per joint
                                                 search phase                CatCMAwM, one category per joint
                                                 swap route                  in place, a respawn at the late start alone
co_optimisation/runners/usd_generator.py       Family.links(spec)            every housing and the biped's sole, phase 5
co_optimisation/utils/update.py                update_articulation_links     a cylinder's radius and height, a body's own placement, phase 5
scripts/rsl_rl/train.py                        --design_search cat           late_start=True, the shared schedule
```

The rule lives in `design_generator`, which owns the specification, and the shelf in the family, so no argument above `usd_generator.py` names an actuator. X5, zero armature on a bare entry, cannot recur, since `is_sim_complete` requires a positive armature, and the construction time warning that named bare entries is deleted with the arguments that made it necessary.

| decision | alternative rejected | reason |
|---|---|---|
| the random phase's actuator draw uniform from the first generation, as the generator already draws it | widening the actuator choice from the donor's picks as the continuous prior widens | the policy meets every actuator from the start and the existing code needs no change, the widening being a decision recorded in section 11 |
| one category per joint over the whole shelf | a shelf per joint class | the audit's B6, a group may pick any unit |
| swap every generation in place, the payload carrying the housings | respawn every generation, the second draft | the second review's item 2, and a respawn rebuilds every environment's prims and its sensors' bindings where the in place route edits one prototype per design, section 9.4 measuring both on the same run |

### 3.3 Edits

B7, `design_generator/common/actuator_catalog.py:282-287`, a new function after `SIM_PARAM_KEYS`.

```diff
@@ -286,6 +286,17 @@ SIM_PARAM_KEYS = (
 )
 
 
+def is_sim_complete(entry) -> bool:
+    """True when ``sim_params()`` states every SIM_PARAM_KEYS key and a nonzero armature.
+
+    apply_actuator_params writes only the keys it is given (co_optimisation/utils/
+    respawn.py:282), so a partial entry inherits the previous design's values, and
+    sim_params emits an armature of zero for an entry that states no rotor inertia.
+    """
+    params = entry.sim_params()
+    return set(params) >= set(SIM_PARAM_KEYS) and params["armature"] > 0.0
+
+
 # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 # Composed actuator (joint-side view)
 # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
```

```diff
     "MOTOR_CATALOG", "GEARBOX_CATALOG", "ACTUATOR_CATALOG", "ACTUATOR_BY_NAME",

     ACTUATOR_CATALOG, ACTUATOR_BY_NAME = _CATALOG.actuator_catalog, _CATALOG.actuator_by_name
     return result
+
+
+def sim_actuator_catalog() -> List["ActuatorLike"]:
+    """The simulation-complete entries of ``ACTUATOR_CATALOG``, read at call time to follow ``set_active_catalog``."""
+    return [a for a in ACTUATOR_CATALOG if is_sim_complete(a)]
```

```diff
     MOTOR_CATALOG, GEARBOX_CATALOG = _CATALOG.motor_catalog, _CATALOG.gearbox_catalog
     ACTUATOR_CATALOG, ACTUATOR_BY_NAME = _CATALOG.actuator_catalog, _CATALOG.actuator_by_name
     return result
+
+
+def sim_actuator_catalog() -> List["ActuatorLike"]:
+    """The simulation-complete entries of ``ACTUATOR_CATALOG``, read at call time to follow ``set_active_catalog``."""
+    return [a for a in ACTUATOR_CATALOG if is_sim_complete(a)]
```

B7, `Family.catalog` returns the simulation-complete shelf and `Family.full_catalog` every entry. Both are in the `Family` hunk of phase 4, which also carries the nominal pose record.

B6, `co_optimisation/runners/usd_generator.py:729-827`, `CatCMAESDesignGenerator`. The overriding `__init__` and both arguments are deleted, so the class inherits `CMAESDesignGenerator.__init__`, and `_resolve_categories` reads the family alone. The class declares no swap route of its own, the payload of phase 5 carrying everything its picks move.

```diff

+    optimised jointly by ``cmaes.CatCMAwM``, which carries a multivariate Gaussian over the

+    ``Family.catalog()``, the latter being the simulation-complete entries only,
```

```diff
+                    assert body in payload, (group, name, body)
```

### 3.4 Implementation instructions

1. Add `is_sim_complete` to the common catalogue module, then `sim_actuator_catalog` to both `actuator.py`, then the `_schema` key to both JSON files.
2. Replace `Family.catalog` and add `Family.full_catalog` with the `Family` hunk of phase 4.
3. Delete `CatCMAESDesignGenerator.__init__` and rewrite `_resolve_categories`, in the same pass as the rest of `usd_generator.py`, whose payload hunks of phase 5 carry the housings.
4. Replace the three tests. `grep -rn "catalog_names\|actuator_groups" co_optimisation scripts` must then return nothing outside `scripts/analysis`, whose `actuator_groups` is an unrelated function of `experiment_params.py`.

### 3.5 Verification and outcome

| check | result |
|---|---|
| shelf per family | `['A1-ABAD', 'A1-HIP', 'A1-KNEE']` and `['TRON1-ABAD', 'TRON1-HIP', 'TRON1-KNEE', 'TRON1-ANKLE']`, host and container |
| full catalogue unchanged | 14 and 15 entries |
| the random phase draws every shelf entry on every joint | `test_the_categorical_random_phase_draws_every_actuator`, 64 designs of generation zero, both families |
| the categorical search starts, runs its random phase and hands over | on the current tree it raises `AssertionError: _generate_individual called before sample_batch` at bootstrap, and after this phase both robots ran a random phase, the late start and two searched generations, section 9.4 |
| a categorical design written in place is its conversion | every body pose, mass, inertia, centre of mass and cooked collider volume within 7.2e-7 of the converted design, in the written and the unwritten environment, both robots, section 9.2 |
| every categorical generation stands at its solved pose, in place | every generation of both robots within 0.006 mm of its record, the late start respawned and every other generation written in place, section 9.4 |

The outcome is a categorical search whose shelf is the family's simulation-complete entries and nothing else, identical for the biped to today's shortlist and newly reachable for the quadruped, which trains its policy across every actuator before it begins to choose among them and swaps its designs in place as the other searches do.

### 3.6 Review against the audit

| audit statement | plan | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| B6, delete both arguments and both attributes, the class inherits `CMAESDesignGenerator.__init__` (section 3) | identical | adhered |
| B6, `_resolve_categories` reads `self.family.groups` and `self.family.catalog()`, refuses fewer than two (section 3) | identical, message naming the catalogue file | adhered |
| B6, the shortlist deleted from `train.py:245` | deleted | adhered |
| B6, one variable per group over the whole shelf, a group may pick any unit (section 3) | unchanged, `cat_groups` is every group | adhered |
| B7, `is_sim_complete` beside `SIM_PARAM_KEYS`, `set(params) >= set(SIM_PARAM_KEYS) and params["armature"] > 0.0` (section 3) | identical | adhered |
| B7, `SIM_ACTUATOR_CATALOG` as a module list in each `actuator.py` (section 3) | `sim_actuator_catalog()`, a function over the live global | divergence, a list bound at import goes stale after `set_active_catalog` (`actuator.py:51-64`), the function preserves the intent through a swap |
| B7, `Family.catalog()` returns the simulation-complete entries and `Family.full_catalog()` every entry (section 3) | identical | adhered |
| B7, record the exclusion in each `actuators.json` `_schema` (section 3) | one `simulation` key in each | adhered |
| X5, absorbed into B7 (section 1) | closed by the positive armature test | adhered |
| the categorical search the shelf serves can run at all | built with the shared late start schedule, its random phase drawing every actuator | addition, section 1 finding 14 and the review's item 5 |
| the categorical search's designs reach the simulation whole | the housings and the sole in the payload, the cylinder in the editor, every generation but the late start in place | addition, section 1 finding 15 and the second review's item 2, measured |

## 4. Phase 3, the fold in the body frames (D1a, D1b, DesignSpec, the committed assets)

### 4.1 Investigation outcome

Both robots stand in a folded crouch, the knee bent and the hip flexed to put the foot under the hip. Where that fold is written decides which joint angle reads zero at the stance, and the two committed assets write it in different places today.

| robot | where the fold is today | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| quadruped | in the joint nominals. The document hangs the leg straight, the thigh body carrying no `quat` and its box running along local -z, and the asset configuration sets the hip pitch to 0.884337 rad and the knee to -1.768673 rad at spawn | `mjcf/quadruped/quadruped.xml:59-64`, the thigh and shank bodies with `pos` alone, `quadruped_identified_cfg.py:93-101`, the nominals, and `quadruped_pointfoot_env_cfg.py:18-24`, the same nominals copied into the plain, HIM and copt tasks at `:40`, `:94` and `:149` |
| biped | in the bodies. The thigh box is laid along `THIGH_DIR_XZ` with a `quat` on the geom and the inertial, the shank body is placed at `l1 * THIGH_DIR_XZ` with `quat` `SHANK_QUAT`, and the ankle housing carries `ANKLE_QUAT`, while every nominal is zero | `biped/builder.py:139-140`, `:352-357`, `:430`, `:436`, `mjcf/solefoot/tron1/base_robot.xml:63-74`, and `solefoot_identified_cfg.py:89-93`, `".*_Joint": 0.0` |

The review's understanding, that the fold is in the body, therefore holds for the biped and not for the quadruped, whose document emits the leg straight and whose fold reaches the simulation only through the configuration. The MuJoCo document of the quadruped carries the same fold in its home keyframe (`quadruped/builder.py:501-502`), and the model predictive control package that consumes that document is not touched by this plan.

#### What the importer does with a body's quat, section 1 finding 1

The finding rests on four places in `/ws/IsaacSim/source/extensions/isaacsim.asset.importer.mjcf/plugins/isaacsim.asset.importer.mjcf/`, the Isaac Sim 5.1 MJCF importer that `MjcfConverter` drives.

1. `MjcfImporter.cpp:1168`, in `CreatePhysicsBodyAndJoint`, composes each body's world transform from its parent's, `myTrans = trans * Transform(body->pos, body->quat)`, so a body's `quat` rotates its own frame and every frame below it.
2. `MjcfImporter.cpp:1301-1302` hands the parent's and the body's world transforms to `addJoints` as `trans0` and `trans1`. In `addJoints` (`:1314`) a joint's frame starts at the joint's position in the body (`:1334`), takes the rotation `computeJointFrame` gives it, which for a single hinge aligns local x with the hinge axis (`:1526-1537`, the rotation at `:1537`), and is carried to world by the body's transform (`:1345`). It is then expressed in the parent's frame and in the body's own frame, `ppose = Inverse(trans0) * origin` and `cpose = Inverse(trans1) * origin` (`:1350-1351`).
3. A hinge is defined at `:1380-1390` through `initPhysicsJoint` (`MjcfUsd.cpp:1109-1135`), which writes `ppose` as `physics:localPos0` and `physics:localRot0` and `cpose` as `localPos1` and `localRot1`, and sets the joint axis to x (`MjcfImporter.cpp:1390`). Since `trans1 = trans0 * body`, `ppose.q` is the body's `quat` composed with the axis alignment, and `cpose.q` is the alignment alone.
4. A body without a joint is welded by `createFixedJoint` (`MjcfImporter.cpp:1356-1361`, `MjcfUsd.cpp:1037-1066`), whose parent side is the body's own `pos` and `quat` and whose child side is the identity.

A body's `quat` therefore lives in the USD as part of the `localRot0` of the joint that carries the body, composed with that joint's `localRot1`, and the rule `localRot0 = quat * localRot1` covers both kinds, the identity being a fixed joint's `localRot1`. A joint nominal, by contrast, lives nowhere in the USD. It is `init_state.joint_pos` of the asset configuration, which Isaac Lab copies into `default_joint_pos` whenever the articulation initialises (`articulation.py:1666-1687`).

#### Why a searched fold must live in the bodies

The co-optimisation search makes the fold a design variable, phase 4 drawing a hip lean and a knee offset for every design. Were the fold kept in the joint nominals, every design would need its own `default_joint_pos`, written per environment at every swap. Isaac Lab reads that tensor in places with different lifetimes, and three of them defeat a per design write.

1. The action term caches it. `JointPositionAction` copies `default_joint_pos` into its offset once, at construction (`/ws/IsaacLab/source/isaaclab/isaaclab/envs/mdp/actions/joint_actions.py:194-195`), so a later write is never seen by the actions, while the observation `joint_pos_rel` reads the live tensor at every step (`envs/mdp/observations.py:219`). After the first swap the policy's zero action would hold the donor's fold while its observed zero followed the design's, so action and observation would disagree by the design's offset.
2. The articulation erases it. `_process_cfg` rebuilds `default_joint_pos` from the configuration at every initialisation (`articulation.py:1666-1687`), and every respawn and every in place update ends in the `sim.reset()` that re-initialises the articulation, so a per design nominal would be lost at every swap and would have to be rewritten after it, together with every cache built on it.
3. The in place route cannot carry it. The editor writes USD attributes of the prototypes the last respawn established (`update.py:82-165`), and a nominal is not a USD attribute, while a body frame is, as `localRot0` of the joint that carries the body.

With the fold in the bodies every design's nominal pose is the zero of every joint, so `default_joint_pos` is zero for all designs at once and never changes, the cached action offset is right for every design, and `joint_pos_rel`, the joint deviation penalties and the reset noise all measure from each design's own stance. The design's fold travels with its document on the respawn route and as `localRot0` on the in place route, phase 5. Only the joint ranges remain, and they are shifted by the constants' fold in the document, so the hardware stops of the donor are unchanged (section 10 records the per design consequence).

#### Every quadruped task under the moved fold, the review's item 7

The six quadruped tasks of this asset, plain, HIM and copt, each Flat and Rough, with their Play variants, were searched term by term for a reader of an absolute joint angle, in `cfg/quadruped/base_env_cfg.py` and in the task classes of `robots/quadruped_pointfoot_env_cfg.py`. The two Go1 asset tasks of the same module spawn Unitree's asset and are untouched.

| term | where | reads | under the moved fold |
|---|---|---|---|
| `joint_pos` action, `use_default_offset=True` | `base_env_cfg.py:134-139` | target = cached `default_joint_pos` + 0.25 action | the cached offset is zero and the joint zero is the crouch, so every target is the same physical angle |
| `joint_pos` observation of the policy, critic, history and HIM groups | `base_env_cfg.py:171`, `:287`, and the HIM groups | `joint_pos - default_joint_pos` | unchanged |
| `joint_vel` observations | the same groups | `joint_vel - default_joint_vel`, the latter zero | unchanged |
| `robot_joint_pos`, critic of the plain, HIM and copt tasks | `base_env_cfg.py:323`, `:533`, `:876`, reading `observations.py:70-76` | `default_joint_pos` itself | its eight pitch channels read 0 where they read 0.884337 and -1.768673, plus in both cases the startup noise of `joint_offsets`. The critic's input, never the actor's, so a policy resumed from an earlier checkpoint acts the same while its value function sees eight shifted constants |
| `pen_joint_pos_limits` | `base_env_cfg.py:1098` | `joint_pos` against `soft_joint_pos_limits` (Isaac Lab `rewards.py:198-201`) | both shift with the document's ranges, unchanged |
| `pen_abad_deviation` | `base_env_cfg.py:1122-1126` | `joint_pos - default_joint_pos` of the abduction joints | the abduction joints carry no fold, unchanged |
| `joint_offsets` startup event | `base_env_cfg.py:996-1004` | adds U(-0.05, 0.05) to `default_joint_pos` (`mdp/events.py:247-283`) | relative, unchanged |
| `reset_robot_joints` | `base_env_cfg.py:1040-1047` | `default_joint_pos` + U(-0.2, 0.2), clamped to the soft limits (Isaac Lab `events.py:1300-1308`) | relative, unchanged |
| the gain, friction and armature randomisations, every other reward, both terminations, every curriculum and command term | `base_env_cfg.py:921-1334` | no joint angle | unchanged |

Isaac Lab's own check that every nominal lies inside the document's limits (`articulation.py:1922-1923`) passes, zero lying inside both shifted ranges. The search confirms the review's expectation for the rewards and the events, and finds one observation that reports the nominal itself, a privileged constant of the critic that section 1 finding 11 already supersedes in the copt task. The run dumps that `scripts/analysis/stats.py` and `dashboard.py` plot record the joint positions, their limits and `default_joint_pos` (`stats.py:55-57`), so a quadruped run after this plan plots its pitch joints about zero where earlier runs plotted them about the crouch.

That the move changes the coordinates and nothing physical was then measured. Appendix C converts the committed document of today and the regenerated one, spawns both, and compares them at twenty random joint configurations, the old one at q and the new one at q less the fold, and again after 200 physics steps under the same targets. The quadruped agrees to 7.2e-7 m in every body origin and centre of mass, 1.0e-9 kg m^2 in every world inertia, exactly in every mass, 1.2e-7 rad in every joint limit after the shift, and to 1.4e-5 m and 1.5e-5 rad after 200 steps, the last being single precision integration of two numerically different but physically equal trees. The inertia of every quadruped body in its own frame, which the observation `robot_inertia` reads, is unchanged, the knee housing being rolled only about its own axis of symmetry.

#### The biped under the same rule

The biped's fold is already in its bodies and its nominals are already zero, so for the biped the review's rule changes only how the fold is written. The Isaac Lab document rotates the thigh's frame by `THIGH_BEND_DEG + 180` instead of laying the box along `THIGH_DIR_XZ` in an unrotated frame, which is the first draft's frame scheme, now the only Isaac Lab document. Every joint keeps its coordinate, the shank and ankle bodies keep their world orientation, and only the thigh's frame turns by 35.0 deg. Appendix C measures the regenerated biped against today's document at the same twenty configurations, with no shift since no nominal changes, to 6.0e-7 m in every body origin and centre of mass, 7.5e-9 kg m^2 in every world inertia, exactly in every mass and every joint limit, and to 7.7e-5 m and 8.3e-7 rad after 200 steps. The thigh's inertia in its own frame, which the plain biped critics and the copt morphology group read through `robot_inertia` (`limx_base_env_cfg.py:290`, `:329`, `:656`), is now diagonal, so those two bodies' eighteen channels change their values while the inertia in the world is unchanged. This rotation is what lets a design change its hip angle by writing one `localRot0`.

#### The quadruped's constants, D1a

D1a stands. The quadruped constants `THIGH_BEND_DEG = 70.0` and `SHANK_BEND_DEG = -154` (`quadruped/constant_params.json:53-54`) are not the crouch the asset trains at, 0.884337 and -1.768673 rad, and `math.radians(50.668778) = 0.884337` and `math.radians(-101.337498) = -1.768673` reproduce it to six decimals. Today they are read only by the MuJoCo keyframe (`quadruped/builder.py:501`), and `GEOMETRY_RULES.md:250-251` states a third pair, 70.0 and -70.0. After this phase they also set the fold of the Isaac Lab document, so they must equal the trained crouch for the regenerated document to be the robot every quadruped task has trained.

### 4.2 Architecture and rationale

```
DesignSpec (both generate.py)
  thigh_bend_offset, knee_bend_offset : float = 0.0       fixed-constant tier, radians about +y
  ankle_bend_offset : float = 0.0                          biped only
  from_dict(data) -> DesignSpec                            unknown keys raise KeyError
  to_dict() -> dict                                        the inverse, shallow
          │
          ▼
builder.pose_frames(spec) -> body rotations, radians about +y, each relative to its parent
          │
          ├── for_isaaclab=True ──► quadruped  thigh quat = THIGH_BEND_RAD + dh
          │   (every Isaac Lab     │          shank quat = SHANK_BEND_RAD + dk
          │    document)           │          pitch ranges shifted by the constants' fold
          │                        └► biped   thigh quat = THIGH_BEND_DEG + 180 + dh, box and inertial along local -z
          │                                   shank at (0, 0, -l1), quat = SHANK_BEND_DEG - (THIGH_BEND_DEG + 180) + dk
          │                                   ankle quat = ANKLE_BEND_DEG + da
          │
          └── for_isaaclab=False ─► the MuJoCo document, unchanged, the quadruped's fold in its keyframe
```

| decision | alternative rejected | reason |
|---|---|---|
| the fold in the frames of every Isaac Lab document of both robots | the first draft's frame scheme only when a spec carries an offset, with a separate copt quadruped asset | the review's item 7, one scheme for both robots and every task, no double fold possible, and no legacy branch in the builders or in `usd_generator.py` |
| `QUADRUPED_IDENTIFIED_CFG`'s pitch nominals zero, `QUADRUPED_INIT_JOINT_POS` deleted | `QUADRUPED_COPT_CFG` and `quadruped_copt.xml` | the dict restated the configuration's own nominals in three task classes, and with the nominals zero it would restate zero |
| offsets are floats defaulting to 0.0, the quadruped's spec having no ankle field | `Optional[float] = None` with a quadruped ankle that must stay None | the review's item 8, a spec always describes a pose, and a field the quadruped must never set is a field it should not have |
| pitch ranges shifted by the constants' fold, not by the per design offset | shifting by the design's own fold | the in place editor would then have to author joint limits per design, the audit's choice, kept, with the defect recorded in section 10 |
| the MuJoCo document keeps the fold in its keyframe | folding it too | its consumer, the model predictive control package, defines its own joint conventions, and nothing in Isaac Lab reads it |
| `from_dict` raising `KeyError` on an unknown field | `DesignSpec(**data)`, which raises `TypeError` | the audit's choice, a named error that lists the unknown fields |

The thigh's `<inertial>` takes `box_quat` unchanged at `:389-392`, so in the Isaac Lab document it is emitted with no quat (`box_inertial_block`, `:206-223`, omits the attribute for None) and the inertia is axis aligned in its link frame, which is what lets the in place editor leave `physics:principalAxes` alone.

```diff
@@ -151,6 +151,22 @@ THIGH_BOX_DIR = np.array([THIGH_DIR_XZ[0], 0.0, THIGH_DIR_XZ[1]])
 THIGH_BOX_QUAT = quat_about_y_from_xz_dir(THIGH_DIR_XZ[0], THIGH_DIR_XZ[1])
 
 
+def pose_frames(spec) -> Dict[str, float]:
+    """Body rotations of the Isaac Lab document, radians about +y, each relative to its parent.
+
+    At zero offsets they reproduce the MuJoCo document's geometry, the thigh at
+    THIGH_BEND_DEG + 180, the shank at SHANK_BEND_DEG absolute and the sole at
+    SHANK_BEND_DEG + ANKLE_BEND_DEG, so the sole keeps that tilt exactly when
+    ankle = -(thigh + knee).
+    """
+    t0 = math.radians(THIGH_BEND_DEG + 180.0)
+    return {
+        "thigh": t0 + spec.thigh_bend_offset,
+        "shank": math.radians(SHANK_BEND_DEG) - t0 + spec.knee_bend_offset,
+        "ankle": math.radians(ANKLE_BEND_DEG) + spec.ankle_bend_offset,
+    }
+
+
```

### 4.4 Implementation instructions

1. Edit the two constants and the `GEOMETRY_RULES.md` paragraph and rows.
2. Add the offset fields, `from_dict` and `to_dict` to both `DesignSpec`, and route both `load_design_spec` through `from_dict`.
3. Add `pose_frames` to both builders and apply the `_leg_xml` hunks.
4. Keep today's two committed documents for the equivalence check, `git show HEAD:environments/environments/assets/mjcf/quadruped/quadruped.xml > /tmp/quadruped_old.xml` and the same for `solefoot/tron1/base_robot.xml`, then run `python scripts/tools/generate_default_mjcf.py`. `git diff --stat environments/environments/assets/mjcf` must report 32 changed lines in `quadruped.xml` and 20 in `base_robot.xml`, and nothing else.
5. Run appendix C on both robots against the two kept documents. Its printed differences must be of the order of section 9.2's.
6. Zero the nominals in `quadruped_identified_cfg.py` and delete `QUADRUPED_INIT_JOINT_POS` and its three uses.

### 4.5 Verification and outcome

| check | result |
|---|---|
| each committed asset is what the shipped specification emits | `test_the_baseline_emits_the_committed_asset`, both robots |
| the Isaac Lab document is the MuJoCo geometry with its fold in the frames | `test_the_isaaclab_document_moves_the_fold_into_the_frames`, every body origin to 1e-6 m, the quadruped's MuJoCo document at its keyframe's fold and the biped's at zero |
| the MuJoCo documents | the biped's identical, the quadruped's different only in its keyframe, now `0.884337 -1.768673` per leg where it was `1.221730 -2.687807` |
| the regenerated documents are today's robots | appendix C, section 9.2 |
| the quadruped donor stands at the trained crouch | 0.292000 m with the foot 0.0001 mm from under the hip axis, forward kinematics of the regenerated document |
| `from_dict` round trip and refusal | `test_design_spec_round_trips_through_a_dict` |
| every task loads, and only the planned fields move | section 9.6, the quadruped's six tasks differing in the two pitch nominals alone outside the copt fields |
| the plain and HIM quadruped tasks and a plain biped task train | two iterations each on the regenerated assets, section 9.4 |

The outcome is one convention for both robots and every task, the fold in the body frames and every nominal zero, under which the co-optimisation search writes a design's pose as body frames and nothing in the asset configuration has to follow it.

| audit statement | plan | verdict |

| `from_dict` raising on unknown keys, `to_dict` shallow, `load_design_spec` through `from_dict` (section 4.6) | identical | adhered |

| quadruped quat on the thigh and shank bodies, emitted under every `for_isaaclab` document (section 4.6) | identical | adhered, the first draft's divergence withdrawn by the review's item 7 |

## 5. Phase 4, `usd_generator.py`, the typed ranges, the renames, the solve and the checks (D1, D1c, D1d)

### 5.1 Investigation outcome

The audit placed every solve and check in `usd_generator.py` and handed the design generator a finished `DesignSpec`, and this phase keeps that division. What changes is the arithmetic. The audit's chains are fitted closed forms, `h = l1 cos(q1) + l2 cos(q1 + q2) + 0.022` for the quadruped and `h = 0.290497 + l1 cos(t) + l2 cos(s)` with `dx = -0.0277 - l1 sin(t) - l2 sin(s)` for the biped. The first is exact only while every clearance of the spec is zero, and the second ignores the shank to ankle clearance (`clearance_shank2ankleAct_x = -0.017221`, `_z = -0.003079`, `biped/variable_params.json`), whose contribution turns with the shank, and fixes the hip axis height and the sole depth at the TRON1 housings, which a categorical search changes (section 1, findings 2 and 3). This phase therefore writes each family's chain from the builder's own placement, `_cluster_layout` and `_housing_dims` for the biped and the clearance fields for both, and takes the foot target from the donor at construction. The chain agrees with forward kinematics of the emitted document to 1.1e-6 m in foot lead and 3.6e-7 m in depth over 400 random designs per robot, and the converted documents agree with it in the simulator (section 9.2).

Because every specification now carries a pose, the hip is solved for every design, there being no legacy document to fall back to. The drawn hip offset is always a lean about the solved hip angle, so the switch the first draft kept for a `"default"` reading is removed (the review's item 10). The knee is rounded to three decimals before the solve and the hip after it, so the only residual of a zero lean is the hip's rounding, at most 5e-4 rad, which the check's tolerance now admits. Without that allowance a search whose pose ranges are both `(0.0, 0.0)`, the way to search the lengths alone, would have every design refused for a residual of a fraction of a millimetre, which the first draft's tolerance did.

The statistics the audit computed on its fitted chains, recomputed on the exact chains over 20,000 uniform draws of the four searched parameters.

| quantity | quadruped | biped | audit, sections 4.4 and 4.5 |
|---|---|---|---|
| foot target ahead of the hip pitch axis, donor | -0.0001 mm | +13.987 mm | 0.0 and +25.2 mm |
| standing height over the box | [0.1924, 0.4050] m | [0.6375, 0.9789] m | [0.194, 0.402] and [0.636, 0.983] |
| total hip offset over the box | [-0.439, +0.576] rad | [-0.356, +0.428] rad | [-0.415, 0.528] and [-0.369, 0.424] |
| ankle offset over the box | none | [-0.343, +0.427] rad | [-0.346, 0.436] |
| worst foot residual | 37.6 mm | 68.4 mm | 36.3 and 68.3 mm |
| tolerance `1.05 (l1 + l2) sin(0.10 + 5e-4)` over the box | [33.7, 56.9] mm | [51.6, 85.3] mm | largest 55.8 and 85.2 mm |
| smallest margin of tolerance over residual, per design | 11.3 mm | 9.3 mm | not computed |
| designs refused, of 3000 | 0 | 0 | not computed |

The tolerance is evaluated per design against that design's own residual, so the comparison that matters is the margin, which stays positive everywhere. The pose at unit length scales with zero lean follows.

| robot | knee offset | hip offset solved | ankle offset | standing height |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| quadruped | -0.25 | +0.125 | none | 0.2456 m |
| quadruped | 0.00 | 0.000 | none | 0.2882 m |
| quadruped | +0.25 | -0.125 | none | 0.3266 m |
| biped | -0.25 | +0.134 | +0.116 | 0.7525 m |
| biped | 0.00 | 0.000 | 0.000 | 0.8044 m |
| biped | +0.25 | -0.133 | -0.117 | 0.8480 m |

The quadruped's unit scale design stands at 0.2882 m and not the donor's 0.2920 because the two decimal rounding of `_spec_from_parameters` takes 0.213 m to 0.21 m, the lattice behaviour D8 records and this plan leaves alone.

The fallback defect of section 1 finding 7 is closed here. With one scheme the fallback needs one record, the last specification that emitted, which the first draft had to keep per scheme. The review's item 9 concerned the removed half of the first draft's `_spec_from_parameters` diff, where `replace(self.baseline, **changes)` was the code being deleted. The new function builds a new `DesignSpec` from the baseline's fields with `from_dict`, and it is shown below whole. The renames are enumerated by `grep -n "_current_scales\|_sample_scales\|_spec_from_scales" usd_generator.py`, which returns `:343`, `:387`, `:390`, `:401`, `:507-509`, `:646`, `:652-653`, `:713`, `:719-720`, `:913`, `:924-925`, and by the same pattern over the repository, which finds no reader outside this file.

### 5.2 Architecture and rationale

```
parameters  ─► _spec_from_parameters ─► the baseline's fields, scales applied and rounded to 2 dp,
(typed ranges)        │                  offsets added to the baseline's zero, picks set
                      ├► solve_nominal_pose(family, spec, dx_target)
                      │     knee rounded to 3 dp, the hip angle that puts the foot at dx_target found by
                      │     bisection on family.chain, the drawn lean added and rounded, ankle = -(dh + dk)
                      ▼
               DesignSpec.from_dict ─► _build_xml ─► check_nominal_pose ─► family.build_xml
                                            │            refused: emit the last feasible spec,
                                            │            record it in _last_emitted
                                            ▼
                                  _build_population ─► links, actuators, specs and Family.nominal,
                                                       all from the spec actually emitted
                                            ▼
                                  Population(..., nominal=[{offsets, dx, depth, sole, height}, ...])
```

| decision | alternative rejected | reason |
|---|---|---|
| exact chain from the builder's helpers, target from the donor | the audit's fitted chains and `DX_DONOR` | finding 2, an 11 mm error in the biped's target and a height wrong for any categorical pick but TRON1 |
| one bisection for both robots on [-1, 1] rad, 60 halvings | the quadruped's closed form `atan2(l2 sin phi, l1 + l2 cos phi)` | the closed form holds only at zero clearances, and the bisection of the same chain covers both robots |
| the hip always solved, the drawn hip offset a lean | `NOMINAL_HIP_REFERENCE` with a `"default"` reading | the review's item 10, and a third of `"default"` draws were refused, the CMA-ES hip coordinate being flat over them |
| two parameter types, `scale` and `offset` | the audit's third, `absolute` | no parameter takes it, and an offset from the baseline's zero is the absolute value for the pose |
| `Family.nominal(spec)` as the one record the runner, the rewards and the checkpoint read | recomputing in the runner | the runner has no access to the builders, and one record keeps the expectation and the measurement of phase 5 on one chain |
| an untyped `(lo, hi)` read as `("scale", (lo, hi))` | refusing it | every untyped range ever passed was a scale, so a caller written before the types is unchanged (rule 2), and `train.py` is typed anyway |
| unknown and retired keys raise at construction | the silent no-op of today | the audit's choice, a louder failure than an ignored parameter |
| the CMA-ES block grows from two dimensions to four, `_check_dimension` refuses an old checkpoint | carrying a two dimensional search on | the audit's choice, and a copt checkpoint from before this plan cannot be loaded anyway, its estimator output widening in phase 6 |

### 5.3 Edits

All of `usd_generator.py`'s hunks except those of phases 1, 2 and 5, in file order. The module docstring and the imports, `:13-36` and `:52`.

```diff
+``"scale"`` (a multiplier on a baseline field) or ``"offset"`` (an addition, in
+the field's own unit). See :data:`DEFAULT_PARAM_RANGES` for every key, its type
+and its range.  A bare ``(lo, hi)`` is read as ``("scale", (lo, hi))``, the only
+type the untyped form ever carried, so a caller written before the types
+existed is unchanged.

+  angle that :func:`solve_nominal_pose` finds for the design's lengths and
+  knee.  Checked by :func:`check_nominal_pose` before the specification is
+  emitted, the biped's ankle offset being computed so that the sole stays flat.
```

The nominal pose machinery, new, after `_quadruped_links` (`:126-151`). The first block of this hunk, the quadruped's `joint_rot` and housing entries, belongs to phase 5 and is shown here because it shares the hunk.

```diff
@@ -148,19 +212,154 @@ def _quadruped_links(spec) -> dict[str, dict]:
                        spec.clearance_shank2foot_y,
                        -l2 + spec.clearance_shank2foot_z),
         )
+        out[f"thigh_{leg}_Link"]["joint_rot"] = {
+            f"thigh_{leg}_Link": _quat_wxyz_y(fr["thigh"]),
+            f"{leg}_calf_joint": _quat_wxyz_y(fr["shank"]),
+        }
+        # Fixed housing sizes (quadruped/builder.py), so a pick moves the masses alone.
+        out[f"abad_{leg}_act"] = _housing(q, act_abad.mass, q.ABD_RADIUS, q.ABD_HALFLEN)
+        out[f"hip_{leg}_act"] = _housing(q, act_hip.mass, q.HIP_RADIUS, q.HIP_HALFLEN)
+        out[f"knee_{leg}_act"] = _housing(q, act_knee.mass, q.KNEE_RADIUS, q.KNEE_HALFLEN)
     return out
 
 
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# Nominal pose: the sagittal chain, the solve and the checks
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+def _rot_y(angle: float, x: float, z: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
+    c, s = math.cos(angle), math.sin(angle)
+    return x * c + z * s, -x * s + z * c
+
+
+def _quadruped_chain(spec, dh: float, dk: float, da: float = 0.0) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
+    """(foot x ahead of the hip pitch axis, foot body depth below the root, sole below the foot body).
+
+    The hip pitch axis is the origin of knee_{leg}_act, which Rules 1 to 4 put at
+    the trunk's mid-height, so only the z clearances move it.
+    """
+    q = quadruped.builder
+    l1, l2 = float(spec.thigh_length), float(spec.shank_length)
+    a_thigh = q.THIGH_BEND_RAD + dh
+    a_shank = a_thigh + q.SHANK_BEND_RAD + dk
+    x = spec.clearance_kneeAct2thigh_x
+    z = (spec.clearance_trunk2abadAct_z + spec.clearance_abadAct2hipAct_z
+         + spec.clearance_hipAct2kneeAct_z + spec.clearance_kneeAct2thigh_z)
+    for angle, vx, vz in ((a_thigh, 0.0, -l1),
+                          (a_shank, spec.clearance_shank2foot_x, -l2 + spec.clearance_shank2foot_z)):
+        dx, dz = _rot_y(angle, vx, vz)
+        x, z = x + dx, z + dz
+    return x, -z, q.FOOT_RADIUS
+
+
+def _biped_chain(spec, dh: float, dk: float, da: float) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
+    """(ankle joint x ahead of the hip pitch axis, its depth below the root, sole below it).
+
+    The hip pitch axis is the origin of knee_{S}_act, whose height _cluster_layout
+    takes from the abduction housing's radius, and the sole's depth follows the
+    ankle housing's, so both move with the actuators a categorical search picks.
+    """
+    b = biped.builder
+    act_abad, act_hip, act_knee, act_ankle = spec.actuators()
+    lay = b._cluster_layout("R", spec, act_abad, act_hip, act_knee)
+    l1, l2 = float(spec.thigh_length), float(spec.shank_length)
+    a_thigh = math.radians(b.THIGH_BEND_DEG + 180.0) + dh
+    a_shank = math.radians(b.SHANK_BEND_DEG) + dh + dk
+    a_foot = a_shank + math.radians(b.ANKLE_BEND_DEG) + da
+    x = float(lay["thigh_pos_rel"][0])
+    z = float(lay["abad_pos"][2] + lay["hip_pos_rel"][2] + lay["knee_pos_rel"][2] + lay["thigh_pos_rel"][2])
+    for angle, vx, vz in ((a_thigh, 0.0, -l1),
+                          (a_shank, spec.clearance_shank2ankleAct_x, -l2 + spec.clearance_shank2ankleAct_z)):
+        dx, dz = _rot_y(angle, vx, vz)
+        x, z = x + dx, z + dz
+    r_ankle, _ = b._housing_dims(act_ankle)
+    bottom = -(r_ankle + 2.0 * b.FOOT_BOX_HALF[1] + spec.clearance_ankleAct2foot_z)
+    _, sole_z = _rot_y(a_foot, spec.clearance_ankleAct2foot_x, bottom)
+    sole = -sole_z + 0.5 * spec.foot_length * abs(math.sin(a_foot))   # lowest corner of the sole
+    return x, -z, sole
+
+
+def _bisect(f, lo: float, hi: float, iterations: int = 60) -> float:
+    f_lo, f_hi = f(lo), f(hi)
+    if f_lo == 0.0:
+        return lo
+    if (f_lo > 0.0) == (f_hi > 0.0):
+        raise ValueError(f"no hip angle in [{lo}, {hi}] rad puts the foot under the hip")
+    for _ in range(iterations):
+        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
+        f_mid = f(mid)
+        if (f_mid > 0.0) == (f_lo > 0.0):
+            lo, f_lo = mid, f_mid
+        else:
+            hi = mid
+    return 0.5 * (lo + hi)
+
+
+def solve_nominal_pose(family: "Family", spec, dx_target: float) -> dict[str, float]:
+    """The pose offsets of *spec*, whose thigh offset is read as a lean about the solved hip angle.
+
+    The hip angle that puts the foot *dx_target* ahead of the hip pitch axis is
+    found on the family's chain for the design's lengths and knee, and the
+    biped's ankle keeps the sole at the donor's tilt. The knee is rounded to
+    three decimals before the solve and the hip after it, so only the hip's
+    rounding remains in the foot's position.
+    """
+    dk = round(spec.knee_bend_offset, 3)
+    hip = _bisect(lambda h: family.chain(spec, h, dk, -(h + dk))[0] - dx_target, -1.0, 1.0)
+    dh = round(hip + spec.thigh_bend_offset, 3)
+    offsets = {"thigh_bend_offset": dh, "knee_bend_offset": dk}
+    if family.has_ankle:
+        offsets["ankle_bend_offset"] = -(dh + dk)
+    return offsets
+
+
+def check_nominal_pose(family: "Family", spec, dx_target: float, lean_max: float) -> None:
+    """Refuse a design whose nominal pose breaks the constraints. Raises ValueError.
+
+    The foot under the hip to a tolerance set by the largest lean a draw may
+    take, the sole flat, the standing height inside the family's corridor, and
+    every pitch joint's nominal inside its hardware range narrowed by the
+    asset's soft_joint_pos_limit_factor of 0.9.
+    """
+    dh, dk = spec.thigh_bend_offset, spec.knee_bend_offset
+    da = getattr(spec, "ankle_bend_offset", 0.0)
+    if family.has_ankle and abs(dh + dk + da) > 1e-9:
+        raise ValueError(f"sole not flat, offsets sum to {dh + dk + da:.3e} rad")
+    dx, depth, sole = family.chain(spec, dh, dk, da)
+    # 5e-4 rad is the rounding of the hip offset to three decimals.
+    tol = 1.05 * (spec.thigh_length + spec.shank_length) * math.sin(lean_max + 5e-4)
+    if abs(dx - dx_target) > tol:
+        raise ValueError(f"foot {1e3 * (dx - dx_target):+.1f} mm from under the hip, tolerance {1e3 * tol:.1f} mm")
+    lo, hi = family.height_corridor
+    if not lo < depth + sole < hi:
+        raise ValueError(f"standing height {depth + sole:.4f} m outside ({lo}, {hi})")
+    for name, value, (r_lo, r_hi) in family.pose_ranges(dh, dk, da):
+        mid, half = 0.5 * (r_lo + r_hi), 0.45 * (r_hi - r_lo)
+        if not mid - half <= value <= mid + half:
+            raise ValueError(f"{name} nominal {value:+.4f} rad outside the soft hardware range")
+
+

     """Everything this module needs to know about one robot family."""
 
-    def __init__(self, name, module, groups, spec_fields, root_body, links):

+                 pose_ranges=None, foot_bodies=(), hip_bodies=(), pitch_joints=()):
         self.name = name
         self.module = module
         self.groups = groups
         self.spec_fields = spec_fields      # actuator group -> DesignSpec field
         self.root_body = root_body
         self.links = links
+        # chain(spec, dh, dk, da) -> (dx, depth, sole) is the sagittal chain of the
+        # emitted document, the bodies and joints are what the runner measures.

+        self.hip_bodies = tuple(hip_bodies)
+        self.pitch_joints = list(pitch_joints)
```

`Family` gains the chain and the measurement attributes, every one defaulted so an existing construction is unchanged, `Family.catalog` and `full_catalog` are phase 2's, and `Family.nominal` gives the record of any specification.

```diff
@@ -170,8 +369,31 @@ class Family:
         return self.module.builder.build_xml(spec, for_isaaclab=True)
 
     def catalog(self) -> list:
+        """The simulation-complete entries, the only ones a design may pick."""
+        return self.module.actuator.sim_actuator_catalog()
+
+    def full_catalog(self) -> list:
         return list(self.module.actuator.ACTUATOR_CATALOG)
 
+    def nominal(self, spec) -> dict:
+        dh, dk = spec.thigh_bend_offset, spec.knee_bend_offset
+        da = getattr(spec, "ankle_bend_offset", 0.0)
+        dx, depth, sole = self.chain(spec, dh, dk, da)
+        return {"offsets": [dh, dk, da], "dx": dx, "depth": depth, "sole": sole, "height": depth + sole}

+
+def _quadruped_pose_ranges(dh, dk, da):
+    # Absolute joint angles, the hardware range being about the folded pose.
+    q = quadruped.builder
+    return (("hip", q.THIGH_BEND_RAD + dh, q.HIP_RANGE),
+            ("knee", q.SHANK_BEND_RAD + dk, q.KNEE_RANGE))
+
+
+def _biped_pose_ranges(dh, dk, da):
+    # Joint sign, the axes being (0, -1, 0), the hardware range about the donor fold.
+    b = biped.builder
+    return (("hip", -dh, b.HIP_RANGE), ("knee", -dk, b.KNEE_RANGE), ("ankle", -da, b.ANKLE_RANGE))
+
```

```diff
+ParamRange = tuple[str, tuple[float, float]]
+PARAM_TYPES = ("scale", "offset")
+
+DEFAULT_PARAM_RANGES: dict[str, ParamRange] = {
+    "thigh_length_scale": ("scale", (0.75, 1.25)),
+    "shank_length_scale": ("scale", (0.75, 1.25)),
+    "thigh_bend_offset": ("offset", (-0.10, 0.10)),     # rad, a lean about the solved hip angle
+    "knee_bend_offset": ("offset", (-0.25, 0.25)),      # rad
+    "abad_stiffness_scale": ("scale", (0.909, 1.091)),

 }
 
-# CMA-ES restricts its search to the two length scales it actually drives.
-CMAES_PARAM_RANGES: dict[str, tuple[float, float]] = {
-    "thigh_length_scale": (0.75, 1.25),
-    "shank_length_scale": (0.75, 1.25),
-}
+# Names rather than a second range structure, so a caller's override of a range reaches CMA-ES.
+CMAES_PARAM_KEYS: tuple[str, ...] = (
+    "thigh_length_scale", "shank_length_scale", "thigh_bend_offset", "knee_bend_offset",
+)
 
-# The DesignSpec field each length scale multiplies.
-LENGTH_SCALE_FIELDS: dict[str, str] = {
+# The DesignSpec field each spec-bound parameter writes. A parameter absent here
+# (the stiffness and damping scales) is consumed by _actuator_params instead.
+SPEC_PARAM_FIELDS: dict[str, str] = {
     "thigh_length_scale": "thigh_length",
     "shank_length_scale": "shank_length",
+    "thigh_bend_offset": "thigh_bend_offset",
+    "knee_bend_offset": "knee_bend_offset",
 }
 
-# The scale key each simulator-side actuator parameter answers to. A parameter
-# with no entry here is passed through from the catalog unscaled.
-ACTUATOR_SCALE_KEYS: dict[str, str] = {
-    "effort_limit": "joint_effort_scale",
-    "velocity_limit": "velocity_limit_scale",
-    "saturation_effort": "saturation_effort_scale",
-    "armature": "armature_scale",
-    "friction_static": "friction_static_scale",
-    "friction_dynamic": "friction_dynamic_scale",
-}
+DEPRECATED_PARAM_KEYS = (
+    "joint_effort_scale", "velocity_limit_scale", "saturation_effort_scale",
+    "armature_scale", "friction_static_scale", "friction_dynamic_scale",
+)
+
+
+def _typed_ranges(param_ranges: dict | None) -> dict[str, ParamRange]:
+    out: dict[str, ParamRange] = {**DEFAULT_PARAM_RANGES}
+    for name, value in (param_ranges or {}).items():
+        if name in DEPRECATED_PARAM_KEYS:
+            raise ValueError(f"parameter {name!r} is retired, see usd_generator's module docstring")
+        if name not in DEFAULT_PARAM_RANGES:
+            raise ValueError(f"unknown parameter {name!r}, known {sorted(DEFAULT_PARAM_RANGES)}")
+        if isinstance(value[0], str):
+            kind, (lo, hi) = value
+        else:
+            kind, (lo, hi) = "scale", value       # the untyped form, which only ever carried scales
+        if kind not in PARAM_TYPES:
+            raise ValueError(f"parameter {name!r} has unknown type {kind!r}, expected one of {PARAM_TYPES}")
+        out[name] = (kind, (float(lo), float(hi)))
+    return out
```

`_build_population` follows the emitted spec and records the nominal pose, the samplers are renamed and typed, `_spec_from_parameters` builds a new `DesignSpec` with `from_dict`, and `_actuator_params` loses the retired loop. `_spec_from_parameters` in full, for the review's item 9, as it stands after the hunk.

```python
    def _spec_from_parameters(self, parameters: dict[str, float], picks: dict[str, str] | None = None):
        """A new DesignSpec from the baseline's fields, the parameters and the actuator picks.

        Not through ``DesignSpec.from_vector``, which drops every fixed-constant
        clearance back to its Python default. Scaled lengths are rounded to two
        decimals, the quantisation the URDF-era generator applied to the box
        extents, and the pose is solved for the rounded lengths.
        """
        fields = self.baseline.to_dict()
        for name, field in SPEC_PARAM_FIELDS.items():
            if name in parameters:
                kind, value = self.param_ranges[name][0], parameters[name]
                fields[field] = round(fields[field] * value, 2) if kind == "scale" else fields[field] + value
        for group, name in (picks or {}).items():
            fields[self.family.spec_fields[group]] = name
        design_spec = self.family.module.generate.DesignSpec
        offsets = solve_nominal_pose(self.family, design_spec.from_dict(fields), self._dx_target)
        return design_spec.from_dict({**fields, **offsets})
```

`_build_xml` checks the pose of every design and falls back to the last design that emitted, and `_lean_max` gives the check its tolerance.

```diff
@@ -441,19 +705,26 @@ class DesignGeneratorBase(ABC):
         """Emit *spec*, falling back to the last feasible specification.

-        run.
+        makes the placement chain degenerate, and ``check_nominal_pose`` when the
+        pose breaks a constraint.  A raised generation would abort training, so
+        an infeasible individual silently repeats the last design that did emit,
+        which costs one individual's diversity rather than the run.  The design
+        actually emitted is left in ``_last_emitted`` for _build_population.

         return xml
 
+    def _lean_max(self) -> float:
+        _, (lo, hi) = self.param_ranges["thigh_bend_offset"]
+        return max(abs(lo), abs(hi))
+
     def _generate_individual_mjcf(self, generation: int, idx: int, spec) -> str:
         gen_dir = os.path.join(self.output_dir, f"gen_{generation:04d}")
         os.makedirs(gen_dir, exist_ok=True)
```

The launcher's literal ranges are typed in phase 1's second `train.py` hunk, the untyped form being equally accepted. `scripts/rsl_rl/play.py:1085-1088` copies two entries of `DEFAULT_PARAM_RANGES` into its `param_ranges`, which are typed after this phase and pass through `_typed_ranges` unchanged, so `play.py` needs no edit for the types.

The tests, `co_optimisation/tests/design_generator_test.py`. `test_length_only_search_stays_two_dimensional` at `:210-216` becomes the four dimensional test and a range test follows it. The nominal pose tests are appended at the end of the file and cover phases 2 to 5, the committed asset and fold tests being phase 3's, the `joint_rot` test phase 5's and the last, of the categorical random phase, phase 2's.
